REMARKs.-During January the highest reading of the barometer was 30.480, on the 9th; and the lowest 29.926, on the 24th. The highest temperature was 75&#39;, on the 25th; the lowest 420, on the 8th, 29th, 3oth, and 3zst; and the mean during month, 58*.9. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 26th, averaging 14.8 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 23 days, measuring 0.8 inch. During February the highest reading of the barometer was 30.520, on the 2nd; and the lowest 30.074, on the 24th. The highest temperature was 790, on the 12th; the lowest 36*.5, on the 2st; and the mean during month, 62*.2. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the z2th, averaging 12.9 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on zz days, measuring 0.3! inch. During March the highest reading of the barometer was 30.444, on the x2th; and the lowest 29.900, on the 5th and 6th. The highest temperature was 83&#39;, on the 25th; the lowest 45&quot;, on the zoth; and the mean during month, 63*.3. S.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 9th, averaging z5.8 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 17 days, measuring 1.87 inches. IX. THE subjoined Notice to Mariners has been received from the Acting Commissioner of Customs at Fusan, Corea:- &quot;COREA. &quot;NOTICE TO MARINERS, &quot;No. 6. &quot;FUSAN DISTRICT. g! (Local Notice to Mariners, No. 2.) &quot;ROCK IN WILLES GULF. &quot;Captain BENZINIUS, of the Corean s.s. Hyenik, having reported the discovery of a rock not marked on the chart, its position, as described by him, is hereby notified: &#39;A rock in Wiles Gulf, in latitude 340 45&#39; N., longitude I270 47&#39; 15&quot; E., at low water, on the 23rd November, at 1.30 P.M., about I foot above water, the north point of an island, name unknown, bearing N.W. by W. I W. The tide was very low this day, as Jupiter passed the moon, and a gale from the N.W. was blowing at the time.&#39; &quot;Captain BENZINIUS believes that &#39;the rock will not be very often uncovered. The name of the place is Chasso Yung, in Fusan district.&#39; &quot; By Order of the Chief Commissioner of Customs, &quot;(Signed) J. H. HUNT, &quot;Acting Commissioner of Customs. &quot;FusAN, I 8th December 1893.&quot; SHANGHAI, ioth January 1894.
1894.] NOTIFICATIONS. No. 283. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. NOTICE is hereby given that Captain R. MORGAN, of the British s.s. Feilung, reports that on the 19th instant he crossed, from north to south, a shoal patch, having from 61 to 7 fathoms, fine sand, for about 3 miles and then deepening to I3 fathoms, blue mud, the position of the middle of the shoal in the line where the Feilung crossed it being-latitude 320 i8&#39; 3o N.1 longitude 1220 27&#39; E., in which locality the Admiralty Chart No. 1,262 shows from 18 to 20 fathoms. 1894. LAPPA. .19 (Received 16th April 1894.) CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, IoT APRIL I894. SIR, I .-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now- transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the first quarter of the year 1894, January-March. b. The Custom House (# , U) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.lkTh 3o and H7.Th 8o per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com- menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow (a ) and Chienshan (&#39; 1b) stations, with three sub-stations, known as the Shekkok (i A), Kuttai (g C), and Kwanchiap (H JR) sub-stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
